The fold incidence: over the wall, sheet-1 and sheet-2 interior cells both attach to the wall cells: the interior 2-sheeted cells over a base region adjacent to the wall attach with TOTAL coefficient ±1 each? Like the height function on S²: the "upper" and "lower" hemispheres both attach to the equator with +1/−1... The fold map structure: locally (s, w, t) ↦ (s², w): π⁻¹(small ball crossing wall) = two "s>0" pieces (s = +√a: sheet +, s = −√a: sheet −) both limiting to the wall s = 0. So 6-cells (interior, 2 sheets) attach onto 5-cells (walls × T²) with incidences that must be determined: this is where the fold geometry enters: e.g., a 6-cell "over base cell B, sheet +" attaches to the 5-cell "over wall-piece W" with coefficient ±1 iff W's closure contains B's... and the sign might differ between sheets! If both sheets attach to the same wall 5-cell with the SAME sign, then... the two 6-cells' boundaries would both include +1·(W-cell): combined effect... vs DIFFERENT signs. This sign pattern is exactly the "new work".
